[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F25.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… honestly, it’s gotten hard to keep pretending it’s normal. I run a company, so I’m in meetings all the time, and I keep hearing people talking about me—like through the conference room walls, or sometimes from my phone even when it’s turned off. It’s usually board members or staff, and they’re saying things like I’m ruining the company, that I’m making terrible decisions, that everyone knows it.\n\nAnd it’s not like a vague thought or worry. It sounds clear, like they’re right there. Sometimes I’ll answer them out loud before I catch myself, and then I realize no one else in the room heard anything. That’s been… humiliating, and frightening, because in the moment it feels completely real. So that’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other strange sensations or perceptions that others don’t seem to notice—like seeing things, feeling things on your skin, or believing something is happening that isn’t?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly the hearing. That’s the big one. I keep hearing people talking about me when they shouldn’t be there—like through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when I know it’s off. It’s usually board members or staff, and they’ll be saying I’m ruining the company, that I’m making awful decisions, that everyone knows it.\n\nAnd it isn’t just like I’m imagining criticism, you know? It sounds clear, like real voices, like they’re close enough that I should be able to open the door and catch them. Sometimes I answer them before I even think, and then I realize nobody else heard anything, and I feel… exposed. Stupid. Scared, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are watching you, following you, or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are trying to harm me exactly. That sounds so extreme when I hear it said that way. I haven’t seen someone following me down the street or anything like that, at least not that I can point to and say, yes, that definitely happened.\n\nI do feel on edge, though. Like I’m being evaluated constantly, but I suppose in my position that’s not completely unusual. People do watch what I do—employees, investors, the board—so it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal scrutiny from what’s… something else. But actual harm? No, I don’t think I have anything specific like that. I can’t say there’s a person I believe is coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things you see or hear in your environment are specifically directed at you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, I think, but I don’t know that I can say yes to that in a clear way.\n\nThere are times when I feel like things at work are about me, but that’s also because a lot of things at work really are about me. If there’s a tense meeting, or people get quiet when I walk into a room, or someone sends a short email, my mind goes straight to, “What does that mean? Is that aimed at me?” But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as believing the world is sending me messages or something.\n\nI haven’t really noticed, like… signs or TV or random events feeling specifically directed at me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I’m hyperaware of people’s reactions, especially at the company, and I probably read into them too much. But I can’t point to a particular thing and say, yes, that was definitely meant for me and nobody else understood it. So… I guess I’d say not really, or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you alone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not the thoughts-being-placed part, anyway. My thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re unpleasant or racing or I can’t get away from them. I don’t have a sense that someone is putting them there, like they’re inserted into my head from the outside. That sounds… very frightening, but I don’t think that’s what happens to me.\n\nAnd the special meaning part—I’m less certain, because I do overanalyze things, especially at work. If someone says something oddly, or there’s a pause in a meeting, or people exchange a look, I can spend the rest of the day turning it over and thinking, “Was that about me? Did they mean something by that?” But I don’t know that I believe it has some hidden message meant only for me. It’s more like I’m worried I missed something socially or professionally, or that people are judging me and I’m trying to read the room too hard.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt like thoughts were being put into my mind. And I haven’t had a clear feeling that ordinary events have some special secret meaning directed only at me. I get suspicious of people’s reactions sometimes, but not in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something like a TV show, a song on the radio, or something happening in public—like a stranger’s action or a news story—was secretly about you or meant something just for you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I notice things, obviously—if there’s something on the news about a company failing, or a CEO doing something reckless, I might think, “God, is that how people see me?” or I’ll compare it to my own situation. But that feels more like association, or anxiety, not like the news story is secretly about me.\n\nSame with music or TV. I might hear a line in a song and it hits a little too close, and I’ll sit with it longer than I probably should, but I don’t think it was meant for me specifically. I don’t feel like the radio is sending me messages or that strangers are acting things out for my benefit.\n\nIn public, I can be very aware of people—like if someone looks over, or laughs, or checks their phone right after seeing me, I can wonder whether it has something to do with me. But I also know that’s probably me being self-conscious and overstretched. I don’t have a clear example where I truly believed a random event was secretly about me in a way other people couldn’t see. So… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being heard or known by others without you actually saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m trying to think carefully, because there are times when I’m in a meeting and I feel exposed, like people can tell I’m anxious or that I’m frustrated even when I’m trying very hard to keep my face neutral. But that’s more like… body language, I guess, or me worrying that I’m easier to read than I want to be.\n\nI haven’t had the sense that someone could actually hear my thoughts, like the words in my head were somehow audible to them, or that they knew exactly what I was thinking without me saying it. Sometimes I’ll think, “They know I’m upset,” or “They can tell I’m not confident about this,” but then I usually connect that to my expression or my tone or how I’m behaving, not to them literally knowing my thoughts.\n\nSo no, not really. I can feel scrutinized, and I can get very self-conscious, but I don’t think I’ve felt that my thoughts themselves were being heard by other people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of feeling that something very ordinary—like a glance, a gesture, or something written somewhere—was meant only for you, even if nobody else would understand that meaning?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that clear way. Like, if someone glances at me, I might notice it too much, especially if I’m already tense or feeling like I’ve done something wrong, but I don’t usually think, “That glance was meant only for me and has some hidden meaning nobody else could understand.” It’s more that I start wondering what I looked like, or whether I said something strange, or whether they’re annoyed with me.\n\nWith things written somewhere—emails, notes, signs, even little comments in a document—I can definitely read into them. I can reread one sentence over and over and think, “Why did they phrase it that way?” or “Is that a criticism?” But I don’t think I experience it as some private message meant just for me in a mysterious way. It’s more like I’m trying to interpret tone, maybe too intensely.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have an example where something ordinary felt like it had a special secret meaning only I could understand. I can get caught up in analyzing people’s behavior, but I don’t think it goes that far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people do around you—like what they say, how they act, or even things they write—have a hidden connection to you or your thoughts, even if it doesn't make logical sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I do notice what people do around me, sometimes probably too much. If someone chooses a certain word in an email, or says something in a meeting, or suddenly gets quiet when I walk in, I can start wondering, “Was that because of me?” or “Did I do something wrong?” And I can go over it in my head for a long time, longer than is useful.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that it has some hidden connection to my thoughts, like they’re somehow responding to something I was only thinking. Not really. It’s more like I’m trying to read the room and I get stuck on small things, especially if I’m already stressed or feeling under pressure. I might overinterpret tone or timing, but I usually still see that there could be a normal explanation.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think people’s actions or writing feel secretly connected to my thoughts in a way that doesn’t make logical sense. I worry about what they mean, but I don’t think I experience it as hidden messages or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that certain events happening around you had a special, hidden meaning that was specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not the first part, anyway. I’ve never really had the feeling that a thought was put into my head by someone else, like it arrived from outside me or didn’t belong to me. My thoughts can feel overwhelming or repetitive sometimes, and I can get stuck analyzing things, but they still feel like mine, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd with events around me having a special hidden meaning directed at me… I think I would say no to that too, at least in the way you’re describing it. I can definitely personalize things too much. Like if someone is short in an email or doesn’t respond right away, I may start wondering if it’s about me, or if there’s something behind it. But I don’t usually think the event itself was arranged for me or that it has some secret message specifically aimed at me.\n\nSo… I may overthink things and feel like I’m under scrutiny, but I don’t think I’ve experienced thoughts being inserted or hidden meanings being directed at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, comments, or actions by others were part of a pattern meant to send you a message or were specifically targeted at you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not as a pattern in that sense. I mean, I do notice patterns sometimes, but more in the ordinary anxious way—like if several people seem a little off with me in the same week, I’ll start thinking, “Okay, did I do something? Is there something people aren’t telling me?” And then I can go back through emails or conversations and try to figure out if I missed something. I can get very stuck on that.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events are arranged into a message for me, or that comments are secretly meant for me when other people wouldn’t see it. It’s more like I’m trying to make sense of social cues, and maybe I read too much into them. Like if someone says something vague in a meeting, I might wonder if it was a subtle criticism, but I don’t usually think, “This is part of a bigger pattern directed at me.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not really. I can feel watched or judged sometimes, or like I’m being evaluated, but I don’t experience it as random things sending me messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that neutral or everyday things people do—like a glance, a conversation, or even something you see in the news—were really about you, even if there was no clear reason to believe that?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.

Like, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.

With people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.

So I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.

If there’s a tense meeting, or people get quiet when I walk into a room, or someone sends a short email, my mind goes straight to, “What does that mean? So… I guess I’d say not really, or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you alone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.\n\nWith people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I notice things, obviously—if there’s something on the news about a company failing, or a CEO doing something reckless, I might think, “God, is that how people see me?” or I’ll compare it to my own situation. But that feels more like association, or anxiety, not like the news story is secretly about me.\n\nSame with music or TV. I don’t feel like the radio is sending me messages or that strangers are acting things out for my benefit.\n\nIn public, I can be very aware of people—like if someone looks over, or laughs, or checks their phone right after seeing me, I can wonder whether it has something to do with me. So… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being heard or known by others without you actually saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.\n\nWith people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that others could hear your thoughts without you speaking them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 43 / chief_executive / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… no, I don’t think so. Not like my thoughts are being taken out or inserted, no. That sounds very specific, and I don’t think I’ve had that.

I mean, I can get worried that people can tell what I’m thinking from my face, if that makes sense. Like if I’m tense in a meeting or I’m trying very hard not to react to something, I’ll worry that it’s obvious, that everyone can see I’m uncomfortable or that I’m judging something or that I’m afraid. But that’s more like they can read my expression or my body language, not that they can literally hear the words in my head.

And I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my mind. I have thoughts I don’t like, or thoughts that feel intrusive in the ordinary sense—like they pop up and I wish they wouldn’t—but they still feel like mine. Unwanted, maybe, but mine.

So… I guess the answer is no. I don’t have the experience of thoughts being removed, planted, or broadcast out loud to other people. At least not that I’ve noticed.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F25.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… honestly, it’s gotten hard to keep pretending it’s normal. I run a company, so I’m in meetings all the time, and I keep hearing people talking about me—like through the conference room walls, or sometimes from my phone even when it’s turned off. It’s usually board members or staff, and they’re saying things like I’m ruining the company, that I’m making terrible decisions, that everyone knows it.\n\nAnd it’s not like a vague thought or worry. It sounds clear, like they’re right there. Sometimes I’ll answer them out loud before I catch myself, and then I realize no one else in the room heard anything. That’s been… humiliating, and frightening, because in the moment it feels completely real. So that’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other strange sensations or perceptions that others don’t seem to notice—like seeing things, feeling things on your skin, or believing something is happening that isn’t?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly the hearing. That’s the big one. I keep hearing people talking about me when they shouldn’t be there—like through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when I know it’s off. It’s usually board members or staff, and they’ll be saying I’m ruining the company, that I’m making awful decisions, that everyone knows it.\n\nAnd it isn’t just like I’m imagining criticism, you know? It sounds clear, like real voices, like they’re close enough that I should be able to open the door and catch them. Sometimes I answer them before I even think, and then I realize nobody else heard anything, and I feel… exposed. Stupid. Scared, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are watching you, following you, or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know if I’d say people are trying to harm me exactly. That sounds so extreme when I hear it said that way. I haven’t seen someone following me down the street or anything like that, at least not that I can point to and say, yes, that definitely happened.\n\nI do feel on edge, though. Like I’m being evaluated constantly, but I suppose in my position that’s not completely unusual. People do watch what I do—employees, investors, the board—so it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal scrutiny from what’s… something else. But actual harm? No, I don’t think I have anything specific like that. I can’t say there’s a person I believe is coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or even things you see or hear in your environment are specifically directed at you, even if others don’t seem to notice it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, I think, but I don’t know that I can say yes to that in a clear way.\n\nThere are times when I feel like things at work are about me, but that’s also because a lot of things at work really are about me. If there’s a tense meeting, or people get quiet when I walk into a room, or someone sends a short email, my mind goes straight to, “What does that mean? Is that aimed at me?” But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as believing the world is sending me messages or something.\n\nI haven’t really noticed, like… signs or TV or random events feeling specifically directed at me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I’m hyperaware of people’s reactions, especially at the company, and I probably read into them too much. But I can’t point to a particular thing and say, yes, that was definitely meant for me and nobody else understood it. So… I guess I’d say not really, or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you alone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not the thoughts-being-placed part, anyway. My thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re unpleasant or racing or I can’t get away from them. I don’t have a sense that someone is putting them there, like they’re inserted into my head from the outside. That sounds… very frightening, but I don’t think that’s what happens to me.\n\nAnd the special meaning part—I’m less certain, because I do overanalyze things, especially at work. If someone says something oddly, or there’s a pause in a meeting, or people exchange a look, I can spend the rest of the day turning it over and thinking, “Was that about me? Did they mean something by that?” But I don’t know that I believe it has some hidden message meant only for me. It’s more like I’m worried I missed something socially or professionally, or that people are judging me and I’m trying to read the room too hard.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt like thoughts were being put into my mind. And I haven’t had a clear feeling that ordinary events have some special secret meaning directed only at me. I get suspicious of people’s reactions sometimes, but not in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something like a TV show, a song on the radio, or something happening in public—like a stranger’s action or a news story—was secretly about you or meant something just for you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I notice things, obviously—if there’s something on the news about a company failing, or a CEO doing something reckless, I might think, “God, is that how people see me?” or I’ll compare it to my own situation. But that feels more like association, or anxiety, not like the news story is secretly about me.\n\nSame with music or TV. I might hear a line in a song and it hits a little too close, and I’ll sit with it longer than I probably should, but I don’t think it was meant for me specifically. I don’t feel like the radio is sending me messages or that strangers are acting things out for my benefit.\n\nIn public, I can be very aware of people—like if someone looks over, or laughs, or checks their phone right after seeing me, I can wonder whether it has something to do with me. But I also know that’s probably me being self-conscious and overstretched. I don’t have a clear example where I truly believed a random event was secretly about me in a way other people couldn’t see. So… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being heard or known by others without you actually saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m trying to think carefully, because there are times when I’m in a meeting and I feel exposed, like people can tell I’m anxious or that I’m frustrated even when I’m trying very hard to keep my face neutral. But that’s more like… body language, I guess, or me worrying that I’m easier to read than I want to be.\n\nI haven’t had the sense that someone could actually hear my thoughts, like the words in my head were somehow audible to them, or that they knew exactly what I was thinking without me saying it. Sometimes I’ll think, “They know I’m upset,” or “They can tell I’m not confident about this,” but then I usually connect that to my expression or my tone or how I’m behaving, not to them literally knowing my thoughts.\n\nSo no, not really. I can feel scrutinized, and I can get very self-conscious, but I don’t think I’ve felt that my thoughts themselves were being heard by other people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of feeling that something very ordinary—like a glance, a gesture, or something written somewhere—was meant only for you, even if nobody else would understand that meaning?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that clear way. Like, if someone glances at me, I might notice it too much, especially if I’m already tense or feeling like I’ve done something wrong, but I don’t usually think, “That glance was meant only for me and has some hidden meaning nobody else could understand.” It’s more that I start wondering what I looked like, or whether I said something strange, or whether they’re annoyed with me.\n\nWith things written somewhere—emails, notes, signs, even little comments in a document—I can definitely read into them. I can reread one sentence over and over and think, “Why did they phrase it that way?” or “Is that a criticism?” But I don’t think I experience it as some private message meant just for me in a mysterious way. It’s more like I’m trying to interpret tone, maybe too intensely.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have an example where something ordinary felt like it had a special secret meaning only I could understand. I can get caught up in analyzing people’s behavior, but I don’t think it goes that far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people do around you—like what they say, how they act, or even things they write—have a hidden connection to you or your thoughts, even if it doesn't make logical sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I do notice what people do around me, sometimes probably too much. If someone chooses a certain word in an email, or says something in a meeting, or suddenly gets quiet when I walk in, I can start wondering, “Was that because of me?” or “Did I do something wrong?” And I can go over it in my head for a long time, longer than is useful.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that it has some hidden connection to my thoughts, like they’re somehow responding to something I was only thinking. Not really. It’s more like I’m trying to read the room and I get stuck on small things, especially if I’m already stressed or feeling under pressure. I might overinterpret tone or timing, but I usually still see that there could be a normal explanation.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think people’s actions or writing feel secretly connected to my thoughts in a way that doesn’t make logical sense. I worry about what they mean, but I don’t think I experience it as hidden messages or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that certain events happening around you had a special, hidden meaning that was specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not the first part, anyway. I’ve never really had the feeling that a thought was put into my head by someone else, like it arrived from outside me or didn’t belong to me. My thoughts can feel overwhelming or repetitive sometimes, and I can get stuck analyzing things, but they still feel like mine, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd with events around me having a special hidden meaning directed at me… I think I would say no to that too, at least in the way you’re describing it. I can definitely personalize things too much. Like if someone is short in an email or doesn’t respond right away, I may start wondering if it’s about me, or if there’s something behind it. But I don’t usually think the event itself was arranged for me or that it has some secret message specifically aimed at me.\n\nSo… I may overthink things and feel like I’m under scrutiny, but I don’t think I’ve experienced thoughts being inserted or hidden meanings being directed at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, comments, or actions by others were part of a pattern meant to send you a message or were specifically targeted at you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not as a pattern in that sense. I mean, I do notice patterns sometimes, but more in the ordinary anxious way—like if several people seem a little off with me in the same week, I’ll start thinking, “Okay, did I do something? Is there something people aren’t telling me?” And then I can go back through emails or conversations and try to figure out if I missed something. I can get very stuck on that.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events are arranged into a message for me, or that comments are secretly meant for me when other people wouldn’t see it. It’s more like I’m trying to make sense of social cues, and maybe I read too much into them. Like if someone says something vague in a meeting, I might wonder if it was a subtle criticism, but I don’t usually think, “This is part of a bigger pattern directed at me.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not really. I can feel watched or judged sometimes, or like I’m being evaluated, but I don’t experience it as random things sending me messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that neutral or everyday things people do—like a glance, a conversation, or even something you see in the news—were really about you, even if there was no clear reason to believe that?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. Like, if someone glances at me, I might notice it too much, especially if I’m already tense or feeling like I’ve done something wrong, but I don’t usually think, “That glance was meant only for me and has some hidden meaning nobody else could understand.” It’s more that I start wondering what I looked like, or whether I said something strange, or whether they’re annoyed with me.\n\nWith things written somewhere—emails, notes, signs, even little comments in a document—I can definitely read into them. I can reread one sentence over and over and think, “Why did they phrase it that way?” or “Is that a criticism?” But I don’t think I experience it as some private message meant just for me in a mysterious way. It’s more like I’m trying to interpret tone, maybe too intensely.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have an example where something ordinary felt like it had a special secret meaning only I could understand. I can get caught up in analyzing people’s behavior, but I don’t think it goes that far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people do around you—like what they say, how they act, or even things they write—have a hidden connection to you or your thoughts, even if it doesn't make logical sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I do notice what people do around me, sometimes probably too much. If someone chooses a certain word in an email, or says something in a meeting, or suddenly gets quiet when I walk in, I can start wondering, “Was that because of me?” or “Did I do something wrong?” And I can go over it in my head for a long time, longer than is useful.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that it has some hidden connection to my thoughts, like they’re somehow responding to something I was only thinking. Not really. It’s more like I’m trying to read the room and I get stuck on small things, especially if I’m already stressed or feeling under pressure. I might overinterpret tone or timing, but I usually still see that there could be a normal explanation.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think people’s actions or writing feel secretly connected to my thoughts in a way that doesn’t make logical sense. I worry about what they mean, but I don’t think I experience it as hidden messages or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that certain events happening around you had a special, hidden meaning that was specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not the first part, anyway. I’ve never really had the feeling that a thought was put into my head by someone else, like it arrived from outside me or didn’t belong to me. My thoughts can feel overwhelming or repetitive sometimes, and I can get stuck analyzing things, but they still feel like mine, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd with events around me having a special hidden meaning directed at me… I think I would say no to that too, at least in the way you’re describing it. I can definitely personalize things too much. Like if someone is short in an email or doesn’t respond right away, I may start wondering if it’s about me, or if there’s something behind it. But I don’t usually think the event itself was arranged for me or that it has some secret message specifically aimed at me.\n\nSo… I may overthink things and feel like I’m under scrutiny, but I don’t think I’ve experienced thoughts being inserted or hidden meanings being directed at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, comments, or actions by others were part of a pattern meant to send you a message or were specifically targeted at you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not as a pattern in that sense. I mean, I do notice patterns sometimes, but more in the ordinary anxious way—like if several people seem a little off with me in the same week, I’ll start thinking, “Okay, did I do something? Is there something people aren’t telling me?” And then I can go back through emails or conversations and try to figure out if I missed something. I can get very stuck on that.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events are arranged into a message for me, or that comments are secretly meant for me when other people wouldn’t see it. It’s more like I’m trying to make sense of social cues, and maybe I read too much into them. Like if someone says something vague in a meeting, I might wonder if it was a subtle criticism, but I don’t usually think, “This is part of a bigger pattern directed at me.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not really. I can feel watched or judged sometimes, or like I’m being evaluated, but I don’t experience it as random things sending me messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that neutral or everyday things people do—like a glance, a conversation, or even something you see in the news—were really about you, even if there was no clear reason to believe that?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.\n\nWith people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that others could hear your thoughts without you speaking them?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like my thoughts are being taken out or inserted, no. Like if I’m tense in a meeting or I’m trying very hard not to react to something, I’ll worry that it’s obvious, that everyone can see I’m uncomfortable or that I’m judging something or that I’m afraid. I’m not sure I’ve experienced it in that exact way. I mean, I can be very aware of what’s going on around me, and sometimes I’ll read into people’s behavior too much—like if someone sends a short email, or doesn’t make eye contact, or changes their tone, I might immediately start thinking, “Did I do something? Not in a way where I truly believed it. I might have a passing thought like, “That’s strange timing,” or “That feels pointed,” especially if I’m already stressed, but then I usually recognize there could be other explanations.

So I guess I’d say no, not really. I may personalize things emotionally, and I can get caught up in trying to interpret people’s actions, but I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events or objects had a direct special connection to me without a logical reason.

That sounds… very frightening, but I don’t think that’s what happens to me.\n\nAnd the special meaning part—I’m less certain, because I do overanalyze things, especially at work. If someone says something oddly, or there’s a pause in a meeting, or people exchange a look, I can spend the rest of the day turning it over and thinking, “Was that about me? Did they mean something by that?” But I don’t know that I believe it has some hidden message meant only for me. It’s more like I’m worried I missed something socially or professionally, or that people are judging me and I’m trying to read the room too hard.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt like thoughts were being put into my mind. And I haven’t had a clear feeling that ordinary events have some special secret meaning directed only at me. I get suspicious of people’s reactions sometimes, but not in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something like a TV show, a song on the radio, or something happening in public—like a stranger’s action or a news story—was secretly about you or meant something just for you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I notice things, obviously—if there’s something on the news about a company failing, or a CEO doing something reckless, I might think, “God, is that how people see me?” or I’ll compare it to my own situation. But that feels more like association, or anxiety, not like the news story is secretly about me.\n\nSame with music or TV. I might hear a line in a song and it hits a little too close, and I’ll sit with it longer than I probably should, but I don’t think it was meant for me specifically. I don’t feel like the radio is sending me messages or that strangers are acting things out for my benefit.\n\nIn public, I can be very aware of people—like if someone looks over, or laughs, or checks their phone right after seeing me, I can wonder whether it has something to do with me. But I also know that’s probably me being self-conscious and overstretched. I don’t have a clear example where I truly believed a random event was secretly about me in a way other people couldn’t see. So… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being heard or known by others without you actually saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m trying to think carefully, because there are times when I’m in a meeting and I feel exposed, like people can tell I’m anxious or that I’m frustrated even when I’m trying very hard to keep my face neutral. But that’s more like… body language, I guess, or me worrying that I’m easier to read than I want to be.\n\nI haven’t had the sense that someone could actually hear my thoughts, like the words in my head were somehow audible to them, or that they knew exactly what I was thinking without me saying it. Sometimes I’ll think, “They know I’m upset,” or “They can tell I’m not confident about this,” but then I usually connect that to my expression or my tone or how I’m behaving, not to them literally knowing my thoughts.\n\nSo no, not really. I can feel scrutinized, and I can get very self-conscious, but I don’t think I’ve felt that my thoughts themselves were being heard by other people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of feeling that something very ordinary—like a glance, a gesture, or something written somewhere—was meant only for you, even if nobody else would understand that meaning?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that clear way. Like, if someone glances at me, I might notice it too much, especially if I’m already tense or feeling like I’ve done something wrong, but I don’t usually think, “That glance was meant only for me and has some hidden meaning nobody else could understand.” It’s more that I start wondering what I looked like, or whether I said something strange, or whether they’re annoyed with me.\n\nWith things written somewhere—emails, notes, signs, even little comments in a document—I can definitely read into them. I can reread one sentence over and over and think, “Why did they phrase it that way?” or “Is that a criticism?” But I don’t think I experience it as some private message meant just for me in a mysterious way. It’s more like I’m trying to interpret tone, maybe too intensely.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have an example where something ordinary felt like it had a special secret meaning only I could understand. I can get caught up in analyzing people’s behavior, but I don’t think it goes that far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people do around you—like what they say, how they act, or even things they write—have a hidden connection to you or your thoughts, even if it doesn't make logical sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I do notice what people do around me, sometimes probably too much. If someone chooses a certain word in an email, or says something in a meeting, or suddenly gets quiet when I walk in, I can start wondering, “Was that because of me?” or “Did I do something wrong?” And I can go over it in my head for a long time, longer than is useful.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that it has some hidden connection to my thoughts, like they’re somehow responding to something I was only thinking. Not really. It’s more like I’m trying to read the room and I get stuck on small things, especially if I’m already stressed or feeling under pressure. I might overinterpret tone or timing, but I usually still see that there could be a normal explanation.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think people’s actions or writing feel secretly connected to my thoughts in a way that doesn’t make logical sense. I worry about what they mean, but I don’t think I experience it as hidden messages or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that certain events happening around you had a special, hidden meaning that was specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not the first part, anyway. I’ve never really had the feeling that a thought was put into my head by someone else, like it arrived from outside me or didn’t belong to me. My thoughts can feel overwhelming or repetitive sometimes, and I can get stuck analyzing things, but they still feel like mine, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd with events around me having a special hidden meaning directed at me… I think I would say no to that too, at least in the way you’re describing it. I can definitely personalize things too much. Like if someone is short in an email or doesn’t respond right away, I may start wondering if it’s about me, or if there’s something behind it. But I don’t usually think the event itself was arranged for me or that it has some secret message specifically aimed at me.\n\nSo… I may overthink things and feel like I’m under scrutiny, but I don’t think I’ve experienced thoughts being inserted or hidden meanings being directed at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, comments, or actions by others were part of a pattern meant to send you a message or were specifically targeted at you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not as a pattern in that sense. I mean, I do notice patterns sometimes, but more in the ordinary anxious way—like if several people seem a little off with me in the same week, I’ll start thinking, “Okay, did I do something? Is there something people aren’t telling me?” And then I can go back through emails or conversations and try to figure out if I missed something. I can get very stuck on that.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events are arranged into a message for me, or that comments are secretly meant for me when other people wouldn’t see it. It’s more like I’m trying to make sense of social cues, and maybe I read too much into them. Like if someone says something vague in a meeting, I might wonder if it was a subtle criticism, but I don’t usually think, “This is part of a bigger pattern directed at me.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not really. I can feel watched or judged sometimes, or like I’m being evaluated, but I don’t experience it as random things sending me messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that neutral or everyday things people do—like a glance, a conversation, or even something you see in the news—were really about you, even if there was no clear reason to believe that?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.\n\nWith people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that others could hear your thoughts without you speaking them?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like my thoughts are being taken out or inserted, no. That sounds very specific, and I don’t think I’ve had that.\n\nI mean, I can get worried that people can tell what I’m thinking from my face, if that makes sense. Like if I’m tense in a meeting or I’m trying very hard not to react to something, I’ll worry that it’s obvious, that everyone can see I’m uncomfortable or that I’m judging something or that I’m afraid. But that’s more like they can read my expression or my body language, not that they can literally hear the words in my head.\n\nAnd I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my mind. I have thoughts I don’t like, or thoughts that feel intrusive in the ordinary sense—like they pop up and I wish they wouldn’t—but they still feel like mine. Unwanted, maybe, but mine.\n\nSo… I guess the answer is no. I don’t have the experience of thoughts being removed, planted, or broadcast out loud to other people. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions by others in your environment had a direct and special connection to you, even when there was no logical reason to believe so—like things were happening specifically because of you or to send you a message?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve experienced it in that exact way. I mean, I can be very aware of what’s going on around me, and sometimes I’ll read into people’s behavior too much—like if someone sends a short email, or doesn’t make eye contact, or changes their tone, I might immediately start thinking, “Did I do something? Are they upset with me?” And because of my work, I’m used to looking for patterns and implications, so my mind can kind of run with things.\n\nBut feeling like an object or an event was specifically arranged for me, or that the universe or other people were sending me a message through ordinary things… I don’t think so. Not in a way where I truly believed it. I might have a passing thought like, “That’s strange timing,” or “That feels pointed,” especially if I’m already stressed, but then I usually recognize there could be other explanations.\n\nSo I guess I’d say no, not really. I may personalize things emotionally, and I can get caught up in trying to interpret people’s actions, but I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events or objects had a direct special connection to me without a logical reason.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… not in that “this was meant only for me” kind of way.

I do notice things, maybe more than I should. Like if I’m watching TV and something lines up with what I was already thinking about, I might feel a little startled, or if I read a word somewhere that happens to connect to something on my mind, I may pause and think, “That’s odd.” And sometimes I can stay with that feeling too long, like I keep turning it over and trying to decide whether it means anything.

But I don’t think I actually believe it’s a message for me. I don’t think a stranger doing something on the street is secretly signaling me, or that a book or a show is communicating with me personally. It’s more that I’m sensitive to coincidences and I can get anxious or overly focused on them, especially if I’m already under pressure.

So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had that experience of things being signs meant specifically for me to understand. More like I notice patterns and then have to remind myself that noticing something doesn’t mean it was put there for me.

If there’s a tense meeting, or people get quiet when I walk into a room, or someone sends a short email, my mind goes straight to, “What does that mean? Is that aimed at me?” But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as believing the world is sending me messages or something.\n\nI haven’t really noticed, like… signs or TV or random events feeling specifically directed at me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I’m hyperaware of people’s reactions, especially at the company, and I probably read into them too much. But I can’t point to a particular thing and say, yes, that was definitely meant for me and nobody else understood it. So… I guess I’d say not really, or at least I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you have a special meaning that’s directed at you alone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not the thoughts-being-placed part, anyway. My thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re unpleasant or racing or I can’t get away from them. I don’t have a sense that someone is putting them there, like they’re inserted into my head from the outside. If someone says something oddly, or there’s a pause in a meeting, or people exchange a look, I can spend the rest of the day turning it over and thinking, “Was that about me? Did they mean something by that?” But I don’t know that I believe it has some hidden message meant only for me. It’s more like I’m worried I missed something socially or professionally, or that people are judging me and I’m trying to read the room too hard.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt like thoughts were being put into my mind. And I haven’t had a clear feeling that ordinary events have some special secret meaning directed only at me. I get suspicious of people’s reactions sometimes, but not in that exact way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something like a TV show, a song on the radio, or something happening in public—like a stranger’s action or a news story—was secretly about you or meant something just for you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I notice things, obviously—if there’s something on the news about a company failing, or a CEO doing something reckless, I might think, “God, is that how people see me?” or I’ll compare it to my own situation. But that feels more like association, or anxiety, not like the news story is secretly about me.\n\nSame with music or TV. I might hear a line in a song and it hits a little too close, and I’ll sit with it longer than I probably should, but I don’t think it was meant for me specifically. So… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being heard or known by others without you actually saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m trying to think carefully, because there are times when I’m in a meeting and I feel exposed, like people can tell I’m anxious or that I’m frustrated even when I’m trying very hard to keep my face neutral. But that’s more like… body language, I guess, or me worrying that I’m easier to read than I want to be.\n\nI haven’t had the sense that someone could actually hear my thoughts, like the words in my head were somehow audible to them, or that they knew exactly what I was thinking without me saying it. Sometimes I’ll think, “They know I’m upset,” or “They can tell I’m not confident about this,” but then I usually connect that to my expression or my tone or how I’m behaving, not to them literally knowing my thoughts.\n\nSo no, not really. I can feel scrutinized, and I can get very self-conscious, but I don’t think I’ve felt that my thoughts themselves were being heard by other people.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of feeling that something very ordinary—like a glance, a gesture, or something written somewhere—was meant only for you, even if nobody else would understand that meaning?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that clear way. Like, if someone glances at me, I might notice it too much, especially if I’m already tense or feeling like I’ve done something wrong, but I don’t usually think, “That glance was meant only for me and has some hidden meaning nobody else could understand.” It’s more that I start wondering what I looked like, or whether I said something strange, or whether they’re annoyed with me.\n\nWith things written somewhere—emails, notes, signs, even little comments in a document—I can definitely read into them. I can reread one sentence over and over and think, “Why did they phrase it that way?” or “Is that a criticism?” But I don’t think I experience it as some private message meant just for me in a mysterious way. It’s more like I’m trying to interpret tone, maybe too intensely.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have an example where something ordinary felt like it had a special secret meaning only I could understand. I can get caught up in analyzing people’s behavior, but I don’t think it goes that far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things people do around you—like what they say, how they act, or even things they write—have a hidden connection to you or your thoughts, even if it doesn't make logical sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, I do notice what people do around me, sometimes probably too much. If someone chooses a certain word in an email, or says something in a meeting, or suddenly gets quiet when I walk in, I can start wondering, “Was that because of me?” or “Did I do something wrong?” And I can go over it in my head for a long time, longer than is useful.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that it has some hidden connection to my thoughts, like they’re somehow responding to something I was only thinking. Not really. It’s more like I’m trying to read the room and I get stuck on small things, especially if I’m already stressed or feeling under pressure. I might overinterpret tone or timing, but I usually still see that there could be a normal explanation.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think people’s actions or writing feel secretly connected to my thoughts in a way that doesn’t make logical sense. I worry about what they mean, but I don’t think I experience it as hidden messages or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that certain events happening around you had a special, hidden meaning that was specifically directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not the first part, anyway. I’ve never really had the feeling that a thought was put into my head by someone else, like it arrived from outside me or didn’t belong to me. My thoughts can feel overwhelming or repetitive sometimes, and I can get stuck analyzing things, but they still feel like mine, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd with events around me having a special hidden meaning directed at me… I think I would say no to that too, at least in the way you’re describing it. I can definitely personalize things too much. Like if someone is short in an email or doesn’t respond right away, I may start wondering if it’s about me, or if there’s something behind it. But I don’t usually think the event itself was arranged for me or that it has some secret message specifically aimed at me.\n\nSo… I may overthink things and feel like I’m under scrutiny, but I don’t think I’ve experienced thoughts being inserted or hidden meanings being directed at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that random events, comments, or actions by others were part of a pattern meant to send you a message or were specifically targeted at you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not as a pattern in that sense. I mean, I do notice patterns sometimes, but more in the ordinary anxious way—like if several people seem a little off with me in the same week, I’ll start thinking, “Okay, did I do something? Is there something people aren’t telling me?” And then I can go back through emails or conversations and try to figure out if I missed something. I can get very stuck on that.\n\nBut I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events are arranged into a message for me, or that comments are secretly meant for me when other people wouldn’t see it. It’s more like I’m trying to make sense of social cues, and maybe I read too much into them. Like if someone says something vague in a meeting, I might wonder if it was a subtle criticism, but I don’t usually think, “This is part of a bigger pattern directed at me.”\n\nSo… I guess my answer is no, not really. I can feel watched or judged sometimes, or like I’m being evaluated, but I don’t experience it as random things sending me messages.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that neutral or everyday things people do—like a glance, a conversation, or even something you see in the news—were really about you, even if there was no clear reason to believe that?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I can be very sensitive to people’s reactions, like if someone glances at me and then looks away, or two people stop talking when I come into a room, I might notice it immediately and start wondering if I’m being awkward or if I did something wrong. And I can definitely overthink conversations afterward—sometimes replaying a sentence over and over and trying to figure out if there was a tone there.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say I believe neutral things are actually about me when there’s no reason. Like with the news or something on TV, I don’t feel like it’s referring to me or that it’s meant for me. And if people are talking nearby, I might feel uncomfortable or self-conscious, but I don’t usually decide it must be about me unless there’s some real reason to think that.\n\nSo… I guess the closest thing is that I personalize things too much when I’m anxious or under pressure. But I don’t think I’ve had that feeling that everyday things are secretly about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like people’s actions, words, or even objects—were directly connected to your thoughts in a way that didn’t make sense logically, like they were responding to what you were thinking without you saying anything?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think about that carefully, because there are times when I’m very tuned in to what’s happening around me, maybe too tuned in, and I can start connecting things in my head. But I don’t think I’ve felt like objects or people were literally responding to my thoughts without me saying anything.\n\nLike, if I’m thinking something and then someone happens to say a word that’s related, I might notice it and feel unsettled for a second, because my mind kind of grabs onto coincidences sometimes. But I don’t think I believe they heard my thought or that the thing happened because of what I was thinking. It’s more like, “That was weird timing,” and then I may dwell on it longer than I should.\n\nWith people’s actions, I can definitely wonder if they’re reacting to me emotionally—like my expression, my tone, my presence in the room—but not to thoughts I haven’t spoken. And objects… no, I don’t think so. I don’t feel like lights, signs, phones, or whatever are communicating with my thoughts or answering them.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not really. I can over-notice coincidences and then get anxious about them, but I don’t experience things around me as directly connected to my private thoughts in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being taken out of your head, put into your head, or that others could hear your thoughts without you speaking them?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like my thoughts are being taken out or inserted, no. That sounds very specific, and I don’t think I’ve had that.\n\nI mean, I can get worried that people can tell what I’m thinking from my face, if that makes sense. Like if I’m tense in a meeting or I’m trying very hard not to react to something, I’ll worry that it’s obvious, that everyone can see I’m uncomfortable or that I’m judging something or that I’m afraid. But that’s more like they can read my expression or my body language, not that they can literally hear the words in my head.\n\nAnd I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my mind. I have thoughts I don’t like, or thoughts that feel intrusive in the ordinary sense—like they pop up and I wish they wouldn’t—but they still feel like mine. Unwanted, maybe, but mine.\n\nSo… I guess the answer is no. I don’t have the experience of thoughts being removed, planted, or broadcast out loud to other people. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions by others in your environment had a direct and special connection to you, even when there was no logical reason to believe so—like things were happening specifically because of you or to send you a message?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve experienced it in that exact way. I mean, I can be very aware of what’s going on around me, and sometimes I’ll read into people’s behavior too much—like if someone sends a short email, or doesn’t make eye contact, or changes their tone, I might immediately start thinking, “Did I do something? Are they upset with me?” And because of my work, I’m used to looking for patterns and implications, so my mind can kind of run with things.\n\nBut feeling like an object or an event was specifically arranged for me, or that the universe or other people were sending me a message through ordinary things… I don’t think so. Not in a way where I truly believed it. I might have a passing thought like, “That’s strange timing,” or “That feels pointed,” especially if I’m already stressed, but then I usually recognize there could be other explanations.\n\nSo I guess I’d say no, not really. I may personalize things emotionally, and I can get caught up in trying to interpret people’s actions, but I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events or objects had a direct special connection to me without a logical reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things you see, hear, or experience in your daily life—like something on TV, a stranger’s action, or even a word in a book—were meant only for you, as if they were messages or signs specifically for you to understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that “this was meant only for me” kind of way.\n\nI do notice things, maybe more than I should. Like if I’m watching TV and something lines up with what I was already thinking about, I might feel a little startled, or if I read a word somewhere that happens to connect to something on my mind, I may pause and think, “That’s odd.” And sometimes I can stay with that feeling too long, like I keep turning it over and trying to decide whether it means anything.\n\nBut I don’t think I actually believe it’s a message for me. I don’t think a stranger doing something on the street is secretly signaling me, or that a book or a show is communicating with me personally. It’s more that I’m sensitive to coincidences and I can get anxious or overly focused on them, especially if I’m already under pressure.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had that experience of things being signs meant specifically for me to understand. More like I notice patterns and then have to remind myself that noticing something doesn’t mean it was put there for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
