But that’s more like they can read my expression or my body language, not that they can literally hear the words in my head.\n\nAnd I don’t feel like someone is putting thoughts into my mind. I have thoughts I don’t like, or thoughts that feel intrusive in the ordinary sense—like they pop up and I wish they wouldn’t—but they still feel like mine. Unwanted, maybe, but mine.\n\nSo… I guess the answer is no. I don’t have the experience of thoughts being removed, planted, or broadcast out loud to other people. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, objects, or actions by others in your environment had a direct and special connection to you, even when there was no logical reason to believe so—like things were happening specifically because of you or to send you a message?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve experienced it in that exact way. I mean, I can be very aware of what’s going on around me, and sometimes I’ll read into people’s behavior too much—like if someone sends a short email, or doesn’t make eye contact, or changes their tone, I might immediately start thinking, “Did I do something? Are they upset with me?” And because of my work, I’m used to looking for patterns and implications, so my mind can kind of run with things.\n\nBut feeling like an object or an event was specifically arranged for me, or that the universe or other people were sending me a message through ordinary things… I don’t think so. Not in a way where I truly believed it. I might have a passing thought like, “That’s strange timing,” or “That feels pointed,” especially if I’m already stressed, but then I usually recognize there could be other explanations.\n\nSo I guess I’d say no, not really. I may personalize things emotionally, and I can get caught up in trying to interpret people’s actions, but I don’t think I’ve had the feeling that random events or objects had a direct special connection to me without a logical reason.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain things you see, hear, or experience in your daily life—like something on TV, a stranger’s action, or even a word in a book—were meant only for you, as if they were messages or signs specifically for you to understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that “this was meant only for me” kind of way.\n\nI do notice things, maybe more than I should. Like if I’m watching TV and something lines up with what I was already thinking about, I might feel a little startled, or if I read a word somewhere that happens to connect to something on my mind, I may pause and think, “That’s odd.” And sometimes I can stay with that feeling too long, like I keep turning it over and trying to decide whether it means anything.\n\nBut I don’t think I actually believe it’s a message for me. I don’t think a stranger doing something on the street is secretly signaling me, or that a book or a show is communicating with me personally. It’s more that I’m sensitive to coincidences and I can get anxious or overly focused on them, especially if I’m already under pressure.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had that experience of things being signs meant specifically for me to understand. More like I notice patterns and then have to remind myself that noticing something doesn’t mean it was put there for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that completely neutral or random things, like something written in a newspaper, a gesture from a stranger, or something happening on TV, were actually about you or had a hidden meaning that was only meant for you to understand?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think that’s basically the same sort of thing you were asking before, right? And… no, I don’t think I can honestly say that happens to me in that way.

I mean, I notice coincidences. If the TV is on and someone says a word I was just thinking about, or I see an article headline that connects to something stressful in my life, I might sort of freeze for a second and think, “That’s weird.” And because I tend to analyze things, I can spend more time than I probably should wondering why it caught my attention.

But I don’t really feel like it’s about me, like the newspaper or the TV or some stranger is sending me a hidden message. I don’t have that sense that it was meant only for me to understand. It’s more like my mind grabs onto things when I’m already keyed up, and then I have to sort of talk myself back down and say, “Okay, that’s just a coincidence, or you’re just noticing it because it’s already on your mind.”

So no, not really. I can overread things emotionally, but I don’t think neutral random things are secretly about me.

Like if I’m watching TV and something lines up with what I was already thinking about, I might feel a little startled, or if I read a word somewhere that happens to connect to something on my mind, I may pause and think, “That’s odd.” And sometimes I can stay with that feeling too long, like I keep turning it over and trying to decide whether it means anything.\n\nBut I don’t think I actually believe it’s a message for me. I don’t think a stranger doing something on the street is secretly signaling me, or that a book or a show is communicating with me personally. It’s more that I’m sensitive to coincidences and I can get anxious or overly focused on them, especially if I’m already under pressure.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had that experience of things being signs meant specifically for me to understand. More like I notice patterns and then have to remind myself that noticing something doesn’t mean it was put there for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that completely neutral or random things, like something written in a newspaper, a gesture from a stranger, or something happening on TV, were actually about you or had a hidden meaning that was only meant for you to understand?\n\nPatient: I think that’s basically the same sort of thing you were asking before, right? And… no, I don’t think I can honestly say that happens to me in that way.\n\nI mean, I notice coincidences. If the TV is on and someone says a word I was just thinking about, or I see an article headline that connects to something stressful in my life, I might sort of freeze for a second and think, “That’s weird.” And because I tend to analyze things, I can spend more time than I probably should wondering why it caught my attention.\n\nBut I don’t really feel like it’s about me, like the newspaper or the TV or some stranger is sending me a hidden message. I don’t have that sense that it was meant only for me to understand. It’s more like my mind grabs onto things when I’m already keyed up, and then I have to sort of talk myself back down and say, “Okay, that’s just a coincidence, or you’re just noticing it because it’s already on your mind.”\n\nSo no, not really. I can overread things emotionally, but I don’t think neutral random things are secretly about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

I’m not sure I’d describe it that way. I mean, I have times where my thoughts feel… overwhelming, or like they’re moving too fast, or I can’t get them to quiet down, especially if I’m stressed. But that’s different from feeling like someone else is literally putting them there.

I know you’re asking it again in a slightly different way, and I’m trying to answer it carefully, because I don’t want to overstate something just because my thoughts can feel intense sometimes.

I don’t really have a clear example of feeling like someone was inserting thoughts into my head or taking them out. That doesn’t sound quite right to me. So I guess my answer is no, not in that literal outside-force way. It’s more that my own mind can feel hard to manage sometimes.
